# Pre-pilot defect fixes — plan

**Status:** in build. **Branch:** `fix/prepilot-defects` (branched from `main` at `eaaa4ec`).
**Read this file first** at the start of every session, then `SESSION_LOG.md` next to it for
what actually happened, then `evidence/` for the classification this plan is derived from.

Fixing the 14 failure buckets found by re-analysing all 62 pre-pilot sessions (11–21 Aug 2026,
13 lawyers, 377 messages). 25 sessions FAIL, 16 carry a DEFECT, 21 PASS.

Full analysis, with the evidence for each bucket:
<https://claude.ai/code/artifact/43d8e63d-1c9d-4460-907c-2d35c1fa3786>

---

## How to use this file

1. Read **Invariants** — they are the things a fix must not break, and two of them are
   counter-intuitive enough that a cold session will get them wrong without reading.
2. Find the first unticked row in the **Ledger**. Rows are ordered by wave; do not start a
   wave before its predecessor is ticked, and do not start a row whose `Depends on` is unticked.
3. Each row is **self-contained**: files, the change, the acceptance test, and the sessions that
   evidence it. You should not need to re-derive anything from the transcripts to execute a row.
4. Follow the **Verification protocol**. A row is not tickable until its acceptance test passes.
5. Append to `SESSION_LOG.md` before you finish. The *Surprises* line is the one that matters.
6. If reality diverges from the plan, follow the **Re-planning protocol** — amend this file in
   the same commit as the work, do not leave the plan stale.
7. **When the user asks** (normally at the end of a session), update the **summary table**
   — the one-line-per-fix tracker shared with Thomas, at <https://claude.ai/artifact/JtrwLwRZnRihfe8kHj3EaJ>. Edit the `ROWS`
   array in `docs/prepilot-fixes/summary-table.html`, then republish that file with the
   Artifact tool, passing the URL above as `url` so the link stays the same. **Every row carries a `sev` (P1/P2/P3, defined on the page: P1 = the lawyer gets something wrong or misleading and cannot tell, or hits a dead end; P2 = correct but incomplete or harder to check; P3 = presentation, unseen wording or tooling).** It grades the defect, not the fix, so it does not change when a row is fixed. **Every Fixed row also carries `fixed: "YYYY-MM-DD"`, shown in the Fixed date column: the date of the first commit in which this file shows the row ticked `[x]` (the date it was accepted, not deployed). Add it when a row is ticked; the page does not compute it.** (Added 2026-09-24 at the user's request, tracker v16.) The table sorts itself by severity, then status, then fixed date (oldest first), so add rows anywhere in `ROWS`; plan order is the tie-break. (~~status then severity~~, changed 2026-09-24 at the user's request, tracker v17.) The progress panel (overall bar, **by severity** since v18, by source) is computed from `ROWS` and needs no editing. (Added 2026-09-23 at the user's request.) **Statuses follow the ledger:** `[x]` is Fixed, `[~]` is In progress, and `[ ]` with confirmed evidence is Verified. **The exception is Partial** (tracker v23, 2026-09-25, at the user's request): a fix attempted and deferred, to be revisited later. **Only the user moves a row to Partial.** The ledger has no marker for it, so a row can be `[~]` or `[x]` here and Partial on the tracker (P4.10 was `[~]` and Partial; Session 29 ticked it, and the user moved it to Fixed in tracker v24).

---

## Invariants

These hold for every row. Breaking one is a worse outcome than leaving a bucket unfixed.

1. **Honest failure is the most-praised behaviour in the corpus and must survive every fix.**
   Three lawyers praised it unprompted, two of them at 5/5 confidence; CambeulW gave 5/5 in
   session 6374 *because* AILA said it could not find something. Every fix to B3 and B5 must
   make the negatives **true** — it must not make the tool more willing to guess. A fix that
   raises the answer rate by lowering the evidence bar is a regression even if the ledger row
   goes green.

2. **Code enforcement beats prompt obedience.** This is already the house style (Phase-2 nudges,
   the parliamentary search budget, the `local_prompt_cache` drafting override). Where a row
   offers a choice between "tell the model to" and "make it so", take the second. B3(b), B7 and
   B12's disclosure footer are all specified this way deliberately.

3. **Do not change retrieval and measure in the same step.** Wave 1 fixes the input to every
   other measurement — while `_matches_jurisdiction` returns `False` for every real extent
   value, no retrieval-quality number means anything. Baseline first (Wave 0), then Wave 1,
   then re-baseline before Wave 3.

4. **A single replay pass is not evidence.** The model is stochastic. Acceptance for any row
   whose test involves an LLM is **n=3, all three clean** for a session classified FAIL, and
   **n=1** for one classified DEFECT (see *Replay configuration* — DEFECT symptoms are mostly
   deterministic, and an ambiguous one gets promoted to n=3). Rows marked *deterministic* have no
   model in the loop at all and need n=1 regardless.

5. **Additive and fail-soft, as everywhere else in this codebase.** No row here justifies a
   breaking change to a stored shape. New columns default; new fields are optional; a failure
   in a diagnostic must never fail the research run.

6. **The lawyers' own words are the ground truth, not the feedback scores.** Two sessions
   (6376, 6361) carry `Q7a = yes` with free text saying the opposite. Where a row cites a
   session, read the transcript, not the score.

## Data handling — read before adding evidence to this repo

`evidence/classification.json` holds session IDs, lawyer usernames, thread titles and my
diagnosis of each session. This matches the precedent already set in `docs/TODO.md`, which
names the same users and quotes their feedback.

**The raw transcript export is deliberately NOT committed.** It contains the lawyers' full
research questions — live Scottish Government casework. It lives at:

```
C:/Temp/aila-prepilot/pre-pilot sessions/session-transcripts-all-time-2026-09-14.csv
```

and can be regenerated at any time from **Admin Portal → Developer → Session transcripts
export** (all time). A session that needs the replay inputs reads it from there.

**Open question for the deploying organisation:** whether the replay set (question text, verbatim)
may be committed as a regression fixture. Until answered, keep it out of the repo and
regenerate locally. See `SESSION_LOG.md` Session 1.

## Verification protocol

Every row states its own acceptance test. Three kinds, and the distinction matters:

| Kind | What it means | n | Where it lives |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Deterministic** | No LLM. A unit test, or a static check over stored output. | 1 | `server_py/tests/` |
| **Replay** | Re-run the original session's question(s) through `/api/system/chat` and assert on the `audit` trace + answer. | 3 | `evidence/replay/` (gitignored output) |
| **External** | Answer comes from a third party, not from code. | — | `SESSION_LOG.md` |

### Iterate on a seam before you pay for a replay (Session 17, 2026-09-21)

**`python -m tools.seam_replay` is the cheap step before an acceptance sweep,
not a replacement for one.** Every fix since P2.2 has been a prompt or
composition change, and P3.1 established that retrieval upstream of them was
already correct — yet the iterate loop was a whole session replay. The tool
rebuilds ONE seam's input from a stored run file, using the product's own
builders, and makes the single model call:

```
python -m tools.seam_replay synthesis --run <run.json> --turn 1 [--without-fix]
python -m tools.seam_replay worker    --run <run.json> --turn 1 [--delegation K]
python -m tools.seam_replay <seam> --run … --dry-run     # payload only, no call
```

Measured on the fixtures it was built against: the **Worker composition** seam
costs **$0.03** a draw against $0.55 for a 6348 replay, and the **Deep Research
synthesis** seam **$0.11** against $0.61-0.79 for the turn. It needs no server.
`--without-fix` rebuilds the seam at an older revision (prompt constant read
from git, pinpoint block stripped), which is what makes an A/B possible.

**What it cannot do:** everything upstream is frozen — the retrieval is
whatever the recorded run retrieved — so it cannot test a retrieval change and
it cannot produce an acceptance. Use it to find the shape of a fix, then spend
on the n=3 replay once. **On a HALTED delegation the Worker seam closes with the
product's own write-up instruction (P3.8, Session 18), so a draw there IS the
write-up round the product makes at the step cap** — that is how P3.8's design
was settled for $0.21 before any code, and how its round is evidenced on
fixtures where a live replay may not halt at all. **`--from-raw` (P3.11,
Session 19) rebuilds each summarised result's appended blocks from its
recorded `raw_result` through `agent_shared.summarised_result_blocks`, the
product's own builder** — the seam replays `final_result`, so a block the
product builds from the raw result (P1.6's URLs, P3.11's outline) reaches it
only this way; the recorded payload is the before-column and `--from-raw`
the after. The pinned configuration runs at temperature 0, so one payload
gives one sample: count draws per payload, not draws. And the seam is an
approximation the other way too: P3.11's one live miss DELIVERED when its
own recorded payload was replayed here — a seam pass is not a live pass.

**The Manager seam (`seam_replay manager`, P3.13, Session 22) must offer the
Manager its tools, and does by default.** Tool-free, it DELIVERED in 3 of 3
draws a payload the live Manager had flattened (`wave3_p313` rep 1 turn 1).
Offered the tools the live call carries, it reproduced the miss in 3 of 3.
The earlier tool-free A/B had reported the fix as 3 of 3 on 6348; with the
tools it was 1 of 3, and the replay agreed with the harsher number. Neither
form is exact. Over the six recorded 6348 turn-1 payloads, each matched the
live outcome in 5. Temperature 0 is also not byte-deterministic on this model:
two draws of one payload came back at 1,466 and 1,488 chars. Take the harsher
reading before paying for a replay.

**Two more lessons from P3.13 (Session 22), for any prompt or answer-seam change.**
(1) **A prompt edit reaches every call that prompt drives, not only the one it
targets.** P3.13's CITATION PRESERVATION clause was meant for the Manager's
answer. It also changed the Manager's FIRST delegation brief, written before
any tool result, and one run went to the wrong Act. A first-round probe (the
system prompt and the question, with the tools offered, capturing the first
`delegate_research` call) separates that effect for a few cents. Probe each
call an edited prompt drives before paying for a replay.
(2) **`replay run --max-spend` is checked between reps, not within one.** A
P4.5 episode costs about $2.40 and 20 minutes in a single turn, so a $4 cap
recorded $5.65. Budget a 6348 n=3 at about $1–1.20 when healthy, and up to
$6 with P4.5 episodes.

**Where the money goes, measured over `wave2`** (41 sessions, $29.55), for
scoping any future sweep: Deep Research turns are **20 of 155 turns and 48% of
the spend** ($0.71 each); 14 turns carry 45%; 8 sessions carry 53%; the
cheapest half of all turns costs $4.16 together. Other levers, none yet built:
replay only up to the graded turn, exclude Deep Research sessions from a row
that cannot touch them, reuse run files when `git diff <rev> HEAD --
server_py/src` is empty (done by hand twice in Session 16), and a cheap model
for plumbing smokes only — **never** for a graded run, where the pinned model
is the one the pre-pilot ran on.

### Replay configuration — decided 2026-09-14, do not vary without saying so in the log

| | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| **Model** | **Pin to `google/gemini-3.1-pro-preview` via OpenRouter** for every baseline and acceptance replay. | All **176** assistant messages in the pre-pilot ran on this model — uniform, no confound. The dev box is currently set to `moonshotai/kimi-k3` with `google/gemini-3-flash-preview` for summarisation; replaying on that measures a different system. `CLAUDE.md` already records model choice as the dominant variable (~10× swing on the same infrastructure). **First session must confirm the model is still served** — it is a preview model and may have been withdrawn. If it has, stop and re-decide; do not silently substitute. **Confirmed served 2026-09-14** (in catalogue, live tool-calling probe returns its own id; `replay.py check` re-runs this). |
| **Model pinning is a DB write, not a request field** | Set `app_settings → provider.openrouter → model`. `replay.py pin` does it and stashes the prior value for `restore`. | `system.py:129` resolves `provider_config.get("model") or body.model`, so the DB row **always** wins and `body.model` is a fallback that never fires. `audit["model"]` reports what actually executed and every run file asserts it against the pin. Sending the model in the request body and assuming it took effect would have silently measured `moonshotai/kimi-k3`. |
| **Summarisation model** | **Left at the dev box's `google/gemini-3-flash-preview` and recorded, not pinned** (user decision, 2026-09-14). | The pre-pilot's value is **not recoverable** — no per-message column records it and that database is not on this machine. Inventing one would be a silent confound in either direction; recording it keeps the gap visible. It is in `runtime_state` on every run file. |
| **Local prompt cache** | **Forced OFF for every replay sweep** (`replay.py pin`); restored afterwards. | It is cross-user and keyed on the raw tool output + canonicalised query, so run 1 populates it and runs 2..n read it back and skip summarisation. The repetitions would stop being independent draws, which is the entire point of n>1 under Invariant 4. `runtime_state` on each run file records the flag state it actually ran under. |
| **Repetitions** | ~~n=3 for the 25 FAIL sessions, n=1 for the 16 DEFECT sessions.~~ **Revised 2026-09-14 (user decision): n=1 across all 41 first, then reps 2–3 only on FAIL sessions whose first pass was ambiguous or disagreed with the frozen classification.** | The rep policy was costed at ~$50; replay now costs ~2.3× the pre-pilot's recorded spend, making a blanket n=3/n=1 pass ~$140 (see *Cost*). Targeted repetition spends the draws where the uncertainty actually is — a session that reproduces verbatim on the first pass does not need three — and keeps Invariant 4's intent, which is that no **FAIL verdict** rests on a single sample. Sessions promoted to n=3 must be named in `BASELINE.md` with the reason. |
| **Cost** | ~**$27** per single full pass *at pre-pilot rates*; **measured ≈2.3× that today**, so ~**$60** for one pass over all 41 and ~**$70–90** for the targeted-n=3 baseline. A blanket n=3/n=1 pass would be ~**$140**, not the ~$50 originally stated. | ~~$50 for the n=3/n=1 baseline~~ — that figure assumed replay cost equals the pre-pilot's recorded per-message spend. It does not: the recorded cost covers only the saved assistant message, while a replay also pays for the Deep Research **planner** call (44 of them in a blanket pass, saved as no message and therefore absent from the CSV) and for whatever extra tool work the current HEAD does. Measured ratio over the first five sessions: 1.04×–8.90×, aggregate 2.34×. Record actual spend per pass in `SESSION_LOG.md` so the estimate stays honest. |
| **Credentials** | OpenRouter key is in the **local DB**, not `.env` — `app_settings` row `provider.openrouter`. `active_provider` is `openrouter`. | Settings are DB-first by design; `.env` holds only startup fallbacks. Change the model via Admin Portal → Developer, or write the `provider.openrouter` row directly. **Restore the dev box's previous model afterwards** (`replay.py restore`) so unrelated work is not silently running on the pinned model. |
| **Auth** | `/api/system/chat` requires `get_current_user` (`routers/system.py:76`). Log in at `/api/auth/login` and send `Authorization: Bearer <token>`. | The July 2026 auth hardening locked every endpoint and there is no global middleware. ~~carry the cookie~~ — the cookie works, but `POST /api/auth/login` also returns the token in the response body and `get_current_user`'s header branch exists specifically for machine callers (`auth.py:118-122` says so). The header is the documented machine path and avoids the `secure`-flag/scheme coupling entirely. |
| **Deep Research needs two calls** | A DR turn must `POST /api/research/plan` first, then send the returned plan as `deep_research_plan`. | `/api/system/chat` returns **HTTP 400** for `chat_mode="deep_research"` with no plan, by design, so a harness cannot bank a standard research run as a DR result. **The pre-pilot's approved plans are not recoverable** (`messages.research_plan`, on a database not present here), so a replayed DR turn runs a freshly drafted plan — adding planner stochasticity the original did not have. The drafted plan is saved in the run file. |
| **Chat mode per turn** | ~~Deep Research from the answer's marker; otherwise the feedback snapshot's `Filter: Chat mode`, else `research`.~~ **From 2026-09-22 (P0.5): Deep Research from the marker; otherwise from the recorded answer's shape — never a default.** | The default was a guess, and it was wrong for 12 of 41 sessions (48 of 155 turns): the export's mode field is blank for the sessions run before it existed (11–13 August), the lawyers ran them in Conversational mode with the Research flag off, and their recorded answers carry the research Worker's headings 0 times in 38. Found 2026-09-22 when the user asked why any turn was Research with the flag off. See P0.5. **Done, Session 20: sources are now `dr_marker` 27, `snapshot` 108, `conversational_marker` 52 and `neighbour` 9 over the 62 sessions, and no turn falls through to a default.** A turn that got no reply takes the nearest answered non-Deep-Research turn of its own session; `DEFAULT_CHAT_MODE` is now `conversational` and is unreachable here. `python -m tools.replay_report modes --dir <D>` prints the per-turn mode and its evidence, the ran-as × answer-shape cross-tab and the export's own answers, and **exits 1** if a guessed mode or a mode mismatch reached the directory — run it on any new sweep before quoting a number from it. **The one field still carrying a default is `research_mode`: see P0.6.** |

**The replay harness is `/api/system/chat` with `emit_tool_details=True`**, which emits the
structured `audit` event (`delegations[] → tools[] → api_calls[]`, with `raw_result` alongside
`final_result`). Spec: `docs/api/AUDIT_TRACE.md`. It exists precisely so a harness can see what
was *retrieved*, not just what was answered — which is what most of these rows assert on.
Do **not** build a new harness against `/api/chat`; it does not emit tool events.

`request_timings.source` is already tagged `"eval"` for this endpoint, so replay traffic stays
separable from live traffic in the Efficiency and Cache tabs. Note the dashboards do not yet
filter on it, so a large replay sweep moves the headline numbers — expected, not a bug.

---

## Re-planning protocol

This plan is expected to change. Two rules:

- **Amend the plan in the same commit as the work that invalidated it.** A session that
  discovers a row is wrong edits the row, adds a `~~strikethrough~~` note saying what was
  believed and what is now known, and says so in `SESSION_LOG.md`. A stale plan is the specific
  failure mode this structure exists to prevent.
- **New findings become new rows, not scope creep inside an existing row.** If executing P1.1
  uncovers a second defect, add P1.5 rather than widening P1.1 — otherwise the ledger stops
  reflecting what was actually verified.

Wave 5 rows are **external** and have unknown lead times. Open them early and in parallel;
they are not blockers for anything in Waves 0–4.

### Wave 1 — The proven code defects (deterministic, no model behaviour change)

| | ID | Row |
|---|---|---|
| `[x]` | **P1.1** | **B2 — Fix the jurisdiction filter.** `_matches_jurisdiction` (`server_py/src/agent/tools/lex.py:44`) splits `extent` on `+` and tests single-letter tokens `E`/`W`/`S`/`NI`. The LEX API returns full territory names. Verified live: the six values the API emits are `['']` (68), `['Scotland']` (42), `['England','Wales']` (4), `['England','Wales','Scotland']` (3), `['United Kingdom']` (2), `['Northern Ireland']` (1) — all six return False for every jurisdiction value. ~~so the filter discards 100% of results~~ **Corrected by the P0.3 baseline, and the correction matters: the filter does not fail closed, it fails *selectively*.** `_matches_jurisdiction` opens `if not extent: return True`, so rows with a **missing** extent pass. Of the **1,009 rows that survived** a jurisdiction filter across the whole baseline, **every one had `extent: []`** and **not one carrying a real extent value survived** — a Scotland filter drops `['Scotland']` and keeps the unknowns (6354 returned the *Building Materials and Housing Act 1945* under `jurisdiction=scotland`). That is why 13 sessions ran with it and nobody reported a broken filter: it returns a plausible non-empty result set that is wrong rather than an obviously empty one. **Measured baseline: 351 of 974 searches (36%) returned nothing to the model, 478 (49%) demonstrably lost rows, ≥2,147 rows lost; 0 of 557 searches lost anything with the filter off.** So the row has **two** jobs, not one: map the real vocabulary, **and decide what `extent: []` means** — leaving unknown-and-include is what makes the failure invisible. `['']` should join it (most SSIs carry it). `uk_wide` currently requires `tokens ⊇ {E,W,S,NI}` and is equally unreachable; decide what it means against `['United Kingdom']`. **Decide explicitly** that UK-wide and assimilated (`eur`) instruments are in scope for a Scotland filter — that is what CambeulW asked for in 6406 and it is the difference between a usable filter and a trap. **Acceptance** *(deterministic)*: unit test asserting True for each live extent value against its own territory, pinned to a fixture of the live vocabulary so an API change breaks the test not the product; plus a `jurisdiction=scotland` search for "Courts Reform (Scotland) Act 2014" returning `asp/2014/18`; plus `replay_report` showing 0 searches emptied by the filter on a re-run of 6354/6381/6408. **Evidence:** 6408 6384 6383 6382 6381 6396 6359 6407 6354 6357. **Depends on:** P0.3 | **DONE.** Policy decided with the user 2026-09-14: the control means **"applies in"**, not "made for" — Scottish-extent + UK-wide + unknown-extent, minus other-jurisdiction id prefixes. Scored over the baseline's 17,560 real rows it drops **0%** of unambiguously Scottish rows and admits **0** clearly non-Scottish ones, against the old code's **97.5% dropped**. Note `['']` had to be treated as unknown-and-include: matching territory names alone would have dropped 40% of Scottish material (2,009 Scottish SIs carry `['']`) — a new trap. The id-prefix tie-break (`nisr/`, `wsi/`) is what makes admitting unknowns safe. **Acceptance passed:** `tests/test_jurisdiction_filter.py` (42 tests, live vocabulary pinned as a fixture); live `jurisdiction=scotland` search for "Courts Reform (Scotland) Act 2014" keeps 20/20 incl. `asp/2014/18`, where the old code kept **0/20**.
| `[x]` | **P1.2** | **B4 (filter half) — Stop the `current_only` filter pretending.** `_INACTIVE` (`executor.py:211–216`) tests for `{repealed, revoked, spent, expired, not in force}`; ~~the API's `status` vocabulary is `final` and `revised` only~~ — **corrected at P2.5: it is `final` 60.3%, `revised` 38.8% and `stub` 0.9%** over 15,160 model-visible rows (`lex_probe --inforce`), three values rather than two, and none of them an in-force signal — so nothing is ever excluded. The field means *which text version is held*, not in-force status, so this **cannot be fixed by extending the word list**. Two parts: (a) remove or relabel the filter so it does not promise currency it cannot deliver; (b) strip in-force assertions from answers — see P2.5 for the model-side half. **Do not** silently leave the toggle in the UI doing nothing. *Open product call when this row is reached:* remove the toggle outright or relabel it to what it actually does. Recommendation is **remove** — a relabelled filter that still excludes nothing is a second trap, and 42 of 62 sessions ran with this on believing it worked. **Acceptance** *(deterministic)*: unit test pinning the live status vocabulary; a search with the filter on returns the same set as with it off, *or* the filter is gone. **Evidence:** 6378 6341 6411. **Depends on:** P0.3 | **DONE — removed, per the user's decision 2026-09-14.** **The row understated this: the filter was not merely a no-op, it was actively asserting currency in two places.** The UI pill read *"In force as at <today>"*, and `build_filter_constraint_block` injected *"Status: In-force legislation only. Do not cite or rely on repealed or not-yet-in-force legislation."* into the system prompt. 42 of 62 sessions ran with that on, so **this is the proximate cause of bucket B4** and it partly pre-empts P2.5 — the model was reporting currency because the system told it the results were current. Removed: the toggle, the pill, the prompt claim, the no-op post-filter **and the request field** (option C, user 2026-09-15). Safe for clients because `AgentRequestBase` does not set `extra="forbid"` — pydantic ignores an unknown key rather than rejecting it, so a client still sending `current_only` gets the same 200 and the value goes nowhere. `audit["filters"]["current_only"]` is deliberately **kept, always null**, so the trace shape is unchanged for the external eval harness and `AUDIT_SCHEMA_VERSION` stays at 1; the natural moment to go to v2 is P2.1, which changes the trace shape anyway. `docs/api/AUDIT_TRACE.md` updated. **Acceptance passed:** `tests/test_current_only_removed.py`; frontend rebuilt.
| `[x]` | **P1.3** | **B5 (data half) — Stop discarding what the search actually found.** Two changes in `executor.py`: (a) `_TYPE_CODES` (`lex.py:80`) has no `eur` entry, so `legislation_type` drops every assimilated EU regulation — confirmed present and retrievable (`eur/2009/1069` returns 10 sections); (b) `executor.py:227` sets `slimmed["total"] = len(results)` **after** post-filtering, destroying the API's real match count. **Baseline: 1,010 of 1,531 searches misreport `total`, including 438 of 557 with *no* jurisdiction or type filter — so this is not a side-effect of B2 and does not go away if P1.1 lands first.** **Measurement warning:** `executor.py` also caps every search to `results[:5]` *after* filtering. That cap is deliberate slimming, not a defect; any "rows lost" figure that ignores it attributes ordinary truncation to the filters and overstates B2 several-fold. `replay_report.RESULT_CAP` encodes it — a search returning exactly 5 is cap-bound and is evidence of nothing. Return `returned` / `total_matched` / `removed_by_filters` as separate fields so the model can tell "5 matched" from "172 matched and your filters removed all but 5". This is the D16 defect-2 fix, now with field evidence. **Acceptance** *(deterministic)*: unit test asserting the three counts are distinct and correct for a filtered search; `legislation_type=secondary` no longer drops an `eur/` result, or `eur` is deliberately excluded with the reason in a comment. **Evidence:** 6406 6408 + the whole of B5. **Depends on:** P0.3 | **DONE.** `_TYPE_CODES` gains `eur`/`eudn`/`eudr` (assimilated EU law sits with secondary — directly-applicable law made outside Parliament), plus `nisro`/`nisi`/`sdsi` which were missing for the same reason. Counts now reported as `returned` / `total_matched` / `removed_by_filters`, with `filters_applied` naming the filters when any removed rows, and `total` restored to the API's real match count. **Acceptance passed:** `tests/test_search_result_shape.py`.

A manufactured URL resolves to a real page and reads to a lawyer as a verified citation, which is worse than a link that misses its provision. | `[x]` | **P1.6** | **B14 residual — enforce the provision URL in code, not in the prompt.** *(New row from P1.5's re-baseline, per the re-planning protocol: P1.4 is done and accepted, this is a separate, newly-measurable defect.)* ~~P1.4 cut manufactured provision URLs from **100% to 19%**… The residual 19% is the model disobeying that instruction where it holds only an Act-level URL — 6365 appends `/section/21` and `/section/22` to `asp/2000/1`, for provisions no tool retrieved.~~ **That premise was the instrument, not the product — the ninth trap, and it was reading a third of the answer.** `_urls_returned_by_tools` asked only whether the URL appeared in `final_result`, which is the **summarised** text whenever summarisation fired — and **70% of section searches are summarised**, the summary keeping the section numbers and dropping every URL. 6365's `/section/21` and `/section/22` **were** returned by `search_legislation_sections`; they survive in `raw_result` and were eaten before the model saw them. Corrected, there are **three** outcomes, not two: *shown* (copied verbatim), *reconstructed* (the tool returned it, the summariser dropped it, the model rebuilt it — substantiated but built by guessing), *manufactured* (no tool returned it anywhere — the provision was never retrieved). Rep-1 like-for-like, Wave 0 → Wave 1: shown 5 → 110, reconstructed 293 → 26, **manufactured 29 → 0**. So the published "327/327 (100%) → 26/136 (19%)" conflated the last two and **understated** what P1.4 achieved. **What P1.6 therefore fixes is the mechanism, not a live wrong answer**: 19% of cited provision links are still *rebuilt* rather than copied, they happen to be right today, and nothing made them so. **Invariant 2 applies exactly as written: stop instructing, start enforcing.** **Built, both halves** (`src/utils/citation_links.py`): **(a) the cause** — `provision_url_block` appends the retrieval's own provision URLs *after* summarisation, for the same reason the Phase-2 nudge is appended there (the summariser cannot eat what it never saw), so the model copies instead of rebuilding; **(b) the guarantee** — `enforce_provision_links` runs at the worker-report seam, the Manager's final-answer seam and the Deep Research synthesis seam, against a **request-scoped** set of every legislation.gov.uk URL any tool returned (harvested from the RAW result, and shared across delegations and DR steps because a provision retrieved in step 2 is legitimately cited in step 5). A provision URL not in that set is **demoted to the Act when the Act was retrieved** and unlinked otherwise, with one footnote. ~~Prefer (b)~~ — both, and (b) is not "a filter over model output" in the drift sense: it is keyed on retrieval provenance, not on wording. **The row's warning held and shaped the design:** the fix must not simply strip links (when only the Act is held, linking the Act is correct — the error is the label naming a provision), and the marker must be **true**: we know no tool returned a URL for the citation; we do **not** know the provision went unread, because a whole-Act `get_legislation_text` carries one URL and no per-section ones. So the footnote asserts only what is known about the link (Invariant 1). **Acceptance** *(deterministic)*: ~~`provision_links_manufactured == 0` over a replay directory, plus a unit test~~ — the first half was **already true at Wave 1** once the detector was corrected, so on its own it would have passed without any code being written. **PASSED on the restated test:** `tests/test_citation_links.py` (28 tests, incl. end-to-end through `run_worker_agent`: identical model output, identical report, retrieved → untouched vs not-retrieved → demoted) plus `tests/test_replay_tooling.py`'s seven new provenance tests, which pin the corrected detector *and* pin that P1.6's own block counts as **shown** — without that last one the fix would have been invisible to its own metric. `provision_links_manufactured == 0` holds over `evidence/replay/wave1` and is now enforced by code rather than observed. **Watch `provision_links_reconstructed` (26 at Wave 1) on the next sweep — that is the number this row moves**, and it needs a Wave 2 sweep, not this row. **Evidence:** 6365 6348 6407. **Depends on:** P1.5 |

Hence the signature in the data: of 34 matches across the baseline, almost every one reads *"is currently in force (status: revised)"* — the model is faithfully reporting the field it was pointed at. **Consequence for scope: P1.2 reduces B4 but cannot clear it**, because the instruction to state in-force status survives the filter's removal — early Wave 1 evidence, 6341 went 3 to 1, not to 0. **And the obvious fix is a trap:** "Jurisdiction & Status" is a *mandatory* section in `_REPORT_SECTIONS` (`agent_core.py:51`), so telling the model to omit it makes `_report_needs_reformat` judge the report malformed and spends an A4 reformat call re-adding the heading — which the model then fills with the same conflation. The fix must change what the Status line is *permitted to say* (explicitly: that in-force status is not determinable from the available sources, and that `status: revised` is not evidence of it), not remove the section. Original evidence: in 6341 it said every provision cited was in force (ss.38–39 Shops Act are repealed) and then **invented an explanation** for the missing text. Nothing below needs re-deriving.** **(1) The three prompt sites have MOVED — P3.5 inserted text above all of them.** They are now `prompts.py:155` (`WORKER_SYSTEM_PROMPT`, *"note if the law applies to the UK, Scotland, or E&W, and if the legislation is in force"*), `prompts.py:282` (`WORKER_SYSTEM_PROMPT_HYBRID`, *"whether legislation is in force"*) and `prompts.py:1096` (`DEEP_RESEARCH_SYNTHESIS_PROMPT`, *"Territorial extent and in-force status where the findings report them"*). ~~111, 236, 1060~~. `_REPORT_SECTIONS` is now `agent_core.py:60`, not `:51`. **`WORKER_SYSTEM_PROMPT_CONVERSATIONAL` carries no in-force instruction at all** — do not assume the symmetry P2.3 and P3.5 have, which is three prompts; this row is three *different* sites, two of them worker prompts and one the DR synthesis. **(2) P3.5 added two in-force instructions of its own and they must not be contradicted.** `prompts.py:102` and `:132` now tell the Worker that *"if the question asks whether something is in force … you MUST call `get_legislation_changes` before answering"*. That is the route; this row governs what the **Status line may say when the route comes back empty**. A fix that forbids in-force statements outright would fight P3.5 and suppress answers that are now properly sourced. **(3) THE FINDING THAT MOST CHANGES THIS ROW: for BOTH of its acceptance sessions the retrieval route comes back EMPTY, so P2.5 remains almost entirely the negative branch.** `ukpga/1998/46` (6411's Scotland Act 1998) returns **857 relations and ZERO `coming into force` rows** — the Act's commencement predates what the change record carries. And 6341's Shops Act 1950 is **not held at all**: `ukpga/1950/28` is a 404 on `/legislation/lookup`, because pre-1963 Acts use regnal ids (`ukpga/Geo6/14/28`), so there is no change record to consult. **So do not design this row around "route to `get_legislation_changes` and report what it says"** — that is right for modern Acts and does nothing for either acceptance session. The bar in the acceptance line — *no in-force claim unsupported by a retrieved commencement source* — is unchanged and is now met by suppression, not by retrieval, on exactly these two. **(4) Commencement coverage is uneven by era and the boundary is NOT established.** Sampled 11 Acts (`/amendment/search`, `search_amended=True`): commencement rows present for 2005, 2009, 2010, 2014, 2018 and 2025 (`asp/2014/18` 258 of 309, `ukpga/2010/15` 647 of 1,245, `asp/2018/9` 184 of 484) and **absent for 1998, 2000 and 2002** (`ukpga/1998/46`, `asp/2000/1`, `asp/2002/3` — 857, 97 and 158 relations, none of them a commencement). **Eleven points is not a cut-off**: the standing rule is that a sparse sample proves nothing about a corpus, and this one is sparse. It is enough to know the route fails for 6411 and to know the Status line must handle both cases. Widen it with `python -m tools.lex_probe --commencement`, whose duplicate/size sections already walk a sample. **(5) One observation worth a measurement, not a design**: `ukpga/1965/35` comes back from `search_legislation` titled *"Shops (Early Closing Days) Act 1965 (repealed 1.12.1994)"* — for some instruments the repeal, **with its date**, is carried in the title string, which `_slim_search_results` keeps and the model already sees. That is a possible cheap partial source for a Status line. **Seen once. Measure how common it is before building on it** — count titles matching `\(repealed [^)]*\)` across the `search_legislation` rows in the replay corpus (`replay_report --dir <dir> corpus` already walks them) before treating it as a route. **(6) Before-column, already captured**: 6411 is in `baseline/` and `wave1/` and says *"Yes, the Scotland Act 1998 is in force"*, naming *"the Scotland Act 1998 (Commencement) Order 1998"* and a date — neither retrieved in that turn. 6341 is in both directories too, is an 8-turn session with Deep Research on turn 7, and is the one that said every provision cited was in force and then invented an explanation for the missing text. **BUILT (Session 9, 2026-09-16); acceptance sweep pending at the time of this commit.** **The row's premise held and THREE of the six facts written into it at the end of Session 8 did not survive measurement — one of them changes the design and one of them is a live defect P3.5 left behind.** **(1) `ukpga/1998/46` does NOT have "857 relations and zero commencement rows".** It has **29 `Commencement Order` relations**, and the reason Session 8 read zero is that its probe filtered `type_of_effect == "coming into force"` exactly. **But the handover's conclusion survives, for a better reason than it gave, and the difference is a trap:** across the **1,355** `Commencement Order` relations the corpus's legislation_ids produce, the `changed_provision` is one of **eight placeholder values** (`specified amended provision(s)` 1,068, `None` 199, `C/O` 73, `specified provision(s)` 11, plus four casing/typo variants and one `Act`) and **never a provision of the subject** — against 5,180 distinct real provisions over 19,031 `coming into force` rows. Those rows mean *another Act's commencement order brought into force an amendment TO the subject*: `ssi/2001/81`, the Adults with Incapacity (Scotland) Act 2000 (Commencement No. 1) Order 2001, appears against `ukpga/1963/41` because `asp/2000/4` substituted words in its s. 90(1). `asp/2000/4` carries both classes (35 real, 14 placeholder), so the split is not academic. **P3.5's block invites the model to state any relation the record lists, citing the instrument named against it** — so left merged, the fix for 6411's unsourced *"the Scotland Act 1998 (Commencement) Order 1998"* would have been a differently-sourced WRONG answer. `_slim_amendment_results` now counts the three classes separately (`provisions_commenced`, `commencement_orders_of_amendments`, `repeal_or_revocation_relations`), marks those groups `commences_this_legislation: false`, sorts them to the back with the self-referential ones so they cannot displace a real relation at `_MAX_RELATED_INSTRUMENTS`, and the block forbids naming them. **(2) The `status` vocabulary is THREE values, not two: `final` 60.3%, `revised` 38.8%, `stub` 0.9%** over the 15,160 model-visible search rows in all seven replay directories. Conclusion unchanged and slightly stronger — none of the three is an in-force signal — but P1.2's row and `test_current_only_removed.py`'s docstring both stated the wrong vocabulary and are corrected. **(3) The row's unmeasured lead (5) resolves as a FLAG, not a date route.** A repeal/revocation marker in the title appears on **258 of 15,160 rows (1.7%), 43 distinct titles**, and a date follows the keyword on only **8 of the 258**. It is worth reading and it is **asymmetric**: present means the instrument is not in force, absent means nothing at all — which is exactly the shape a Status line needs, and which the first draft of this row's own detector got wrong in the flattering direction (it graded 6411's *"Yes, the Scotland Act 1998 is in force"* as SOURCED because an unrelated repeal-marked row appeared in the same turn's search results). **(4) And a fourth lead, chased and closed: the effect STRING sometimes carries a date** — `saved (6.5.1999)`, `amended (1.7.1999)`, `repealed (1.1.1996)` — so P3.5's flat "there is no DATE on any relation" looked wrong. Measured over all 272 corpus legislation_ids in both directions: **552 of 89,465 relations (0.6%) embed a date, and ZERO of the 19,031 `coming into force` rows do.** P3.5's statement stands exactly where it matters, the second hop is still required, and the next session does not need to re-chase this. **THE FIX, at the four seams `src/utils/search_scope.py` owns, plus the slimmer and the prompts:** **(a) the affordance is removed, not argued with** — `_slim_search_results` emits `status` as **`text_version`**. Of `wave1`'s 65 in-force assertion sentences, **48 cite the text version as the evidence** (*"is currently in force (revised)"*, *"Status: Revised (In force)"*); the model was not inventing a source, it was quoting the only field that looked like one. `extract_sources` reads the new key with a `status` fallback, so the Sources rail is byte-identical. **(b) one sentence on the search block** — kept to one because it rides on 790 searches a sweep and `test_a_long_query_is_capped_not_dropped` bounds the block at 2,000 chars (the first draft broke it). **(c) `currency_note`** — a `[CURRENCY …]` block naming the repeal-marked titles on the 1.7% of pages that have one, and silent on the rest. **(d) `record_currency` + `_currency_limb`** on the worker's report block, which is **the one limb here that speaks when its step found nothing** — the opposite gate from `_enabling_limb` and `_relations_limb`, because a step that established nothing about currency is exactly the step whose report says *"all cited legislation is currently in force"*, and the Manager writing that sentence has seen no tool result. **(e) `_currency_footer_clause`** on the lawyer-facing footer, gated on the structural fact rather than a prose detector, in both branches. **(f) the strip widened to `CURRENCY`**, test written first — the live defect P2.3 hit with `[ENABLING POWER …]`. **(g) `_IN_FORCE_RULE` on all THREE legislation worker prompts and a rewritten Status line at all three sites.** The row warned against assuming P2.3's and P3.5's three-prompt symmetry; that was right about the Status *lines* (three different sites, two worker prompts and the DR synthesis) and wrong about the *rule*: **`WORKER_SYSTEM_PROMPT_CONVERSATIONAL` had no in-force instruction, and `get_worker_system_prompt` returns it whenever `_chat_mode == "conversational"` — which is the mode 6411 ran in.** The site with no instruction was the site with the defect. **The mandatory section is kept** (the A4 trap the row flagged) and what it may *say* changed: territorial extent from the metadata; currency only from a retrieved `coming into force` or repeal relation, a title marker, or `valid_date`; otherwise "not verified". **`valid_date` on `/legislation/text` is the substitution that keeps this on the right side of Invariant 1** — `2026-03-11` for the Scotland Act 1998, the date the held revised text is up to date to, available on the 18% of turns that reach Phase 3 and rising under P3.5. Not an in-force date, and the honest thing a Status line can say instead of nothing. **THE INSTRUMENT, and the old one is deliberately left alone.** `replay_report currency` grades per turn on one prose test against one structural fact read off the audit trace, so the headline cannot be moved by a wording change in the product (P2.2's denominator was). **`IN_FORCE_CLAIM` — which produced the published 27 -> 30 series — is byte-identical, and it undercounts by 30%**: it requires "is/are/remains/currently in force" and so catches none of the bare Status bullets that are the purest form of the defect (`In force (revised).` 6341 x3, 6384 x6, 6389 x3; `Status: Revised (In force).` 6406 x4). Properly counted `wave1` is **43 turns / 65 assertions**, not 30 / 34. Both are printed. **`SOURCED` beside `UNSUPPORTED` is the suppression check and must not reach zero** — this row's specific risk is the inverse of P3.5's. Validated in both directions on all 86 `in force` sentences in `wave1` (37 pinned strings in `tests/test_in_force_status.py`), and re-run over all seven historical directories: `baseline/` 54 unsupported, `wave1/` 43, `wave2_p21/` 7, `wave2_p22/` 4, `wave2_p22_final/` 5, `wave2_p23/` 10, and **`wave3_p35/` 0 unsupported with 4 sourced** — P3.5's own sessions already show the shape this row wants where its route works, and 2 of those 4 still cite a text version (*"SSI 2020/475 is in force (status: revised)"*), which is the residual this row removes. `python -m tools.lex_probe --inforce [--full]` reprints every figure above. **922 tests** (833 -> 922). | **ACCEPTANCE PASSED** (`evidence/replay/wave2_p25/`, 8 runs - 6341 and 6411 at n=3, plus **6409 and 6383 at n=1 which are in the sweep solely for the suppression check**; 42 answered turns, **$9.76**, ~2 h, zero model mismatches - and `evidence/replay/wave2_p25b/` for 6411 re-run on a strengthened prompt, $0.90). Graded per TURN with `replay_report currency`. **Turns asserting currency with NO commencement relation retrieved went 7 of 24 to 0 of 42; assertions citing a text version as the evidence 13 to 0.** **The suppression check is the number this row could most easily have faked, and P3.5's win survived intact**: the commencement relation was still delivered in **6 of 6** in-scope turns (`wave1` 0 of 8), with zero denials - 6341 asks definition questions and 6411's Act has no `coming into force` relation at all, so `sourced` is legitimately 0 on both and a fix that silenced the answers the material supports would have looked identical on those two alone. **Invariant 1: 18 of 24 matched turn slots grew**, tool calls per answered turn 15.5 to 15.2; the six that shrank were each read and **none is currency suppression** - a declined duplicate question that correctly redirected on mode, the blank report below, one turn that is **shorter and strictly better** (6409 t7: a false negative replaced by the right answer, which is P2.4's failure removed), and three clarifying questions that `wave3_p35` asked identically before this row. **Stated costs, not buried:** **10 of 30** turns whose question never mentioned currency carry a disclaimer anyway (33%, `currency --unasked`; ~~3 of 8 in one rep~~ was quoted before the measure had a command behind it), while only 1 of the 11 that DID ask carries one because the rest got a sourced answer (the price of `_currency_limb` speaking unconditionally, and the right trade given the section is mandatory and previously said *"All referenced legislation is currently in force"* about a session citing an Act whose ss. 38-39 are repealed); the limb costs 873 characters on every legislation worker report; on 6341 rep 1 cost went $2.09 to $2.24 (+7%) while tool calls fell 198 to 180 and the mean answer grew 4,205 to 6,245 chars. **A second directory was needed and the reason is recorded**: 6411 obeyed the *report what the record holds* clause in **1 of 3** reps, so one line was added to the conversational PHASE 2b and 6411 re-run - **2 of 3 reporting the repeals, mean 741 chars against `wave1`'s 480**, and rep 1 retrieved the actual Commencement Order 1998. **THE INSTRUMENT WAS WRONG EIGHT TIMES, in both directions**, five of them found by reading the after-column of a live run where the model wrote exactly what the product now asks for; the last four all read the FIXED behaviour as the defect, so the after-column ran pessimistic rather than flattering. Full list in `SESSION_LOG.md` Session 9. After every correction, re-run over all seven historical directories: **not one before-column number moved.** **957 tests** (833 -> 957). Full numbers: `BASELINE.md`, *B4*. **Instrument note from the Wave 2 re-baseline (Session 16): `summary`/`compare`'s "unsupported in-force claims" is now CIRCULAR.** It runs the old `IN_FORCE_CLAIM` over the whole answer, and this row's own lawyer-facing disclaimer trips it: `compare --before wave1 --after wave2` prints **30 -> 61**, while this row's grader `currency` reads the model's prose as UNSUPPORTED **43 -> 3** (assertion sentences 66 -> 4; the old detector on the prose alone 30 -> 6 turns). Quote `currency`, never `compare`, for B4. Same shape as the `HALT_PARAPHRASE` hazard.
| `[x]` | **P2.6** | **The A4 reformat retry fires on halted workers.** *(New row, from P2.1's baseline replay — not scope creep inside P2.1: different code site, different cost, and it would survive a complete fix to the halt presentation.)* In 6340 the worker halted with no findings, `_report_needs_reformat` saw a report with no section headers, and `_reformat_worker_report` spent a no-tools LLM call producing a perfectly-structured empty report — *"Jurisdiction & Status: Not applicable (no research generated). References: None found."* A halted worker has nothing to reformat, so the retry is pure cost and it **launders a failure into something that looks like a finished report**, which is what lets the halt read as a legal answer downstream. Short-circuit: skip the reformat when the worker report is a halt (or is empty), and count it. **Note it also pollutes the P1.5/Wave-3 measurement** — `report_reformat_retries` and the Efficiency tab's `reformat_rate` currently score these as prompt-adherence failures when the model never had a report to format. **Acceptance** *(deterministic)*: unit test — a halt-only worker report produces no reformat call and increments no `report_reformat_retries`. **Evidence:** 6340; re-count across the baseline. **Depends on:** P2.1 | **DONE (Session 5).** One-line guard in `run_worker_agent`, keyed on P2.1's new `result["halted"]` — which is why this row depended on P2.1 rather than duplicating its detection. Deliberately narrow: an unhalted malformed report still gets its retry, and there is a test for that, because disabling A4 wholesale would trade one defect for another. **Acceptance passed** (`tests/test_research_halt.py`): a halted worker makes exactly one LLM call and increments no `report_reformat_retries`; an unhalted malformed one still makes two and increments one. Note the correctness half was already closed by P2.1's overwrite — this row is the wasted call and the polluted `reformat_rate`.

The parliamentary budget exists for exactly the second shape and the legislation bot never got one. **Note the interaction with D5's tool memo:** an exact-arg repeat is already free (served from the memo) and still counted redundant, so the 26% is loop *behaviour*, not spend — the cost is the ReAct rounds it burns, which is what pushes a run into the cap. **Do NOT simply copy `{"remaining": 3}`** — the legislation Phase-1/Phase-2 shape is different from Holyrood's and the right number must come from the distribution (median Phase-1 calls per turn, not a guess). **Acceptance** *(deterministic + replay)*: a unit test that the budget hard-stops discovery and does not touch Phase-2 retrieval, plus `react_turns_max` at-cap falling on a re-sweep without `sources_kept` falling. **Evidence:** 6409 6335 6382 6338. **Depends on:** P2.1 | **ONE MORE INSTANCE FOR THIS FAMILY, found at P2.5 (Session 9) and deliberately not given its own row.** 6341 turn 8 asks *"what do you mean when you say legislation is noted as a stub"* - a meta-question about the index - and the answer explains stub records, which instruments tend to be stubs, and what to consult instead. The step cap is `chat_loop(max_turns=20)` (`openrouter_client.py:157-179`, `ollama_client.py:64-100`), returning `halted={reason, limit, steps}`. **It counts ROUNDS; a budget counts CALLS**, and the model batches: `wave2_p28/6341 r1 t7` step 2 made 41 calls in 20 rounds. Each delegation and each Deep Research step is its own worker run with its own budget; the tool memo is per request and shared across steps. **(b) The hazard, and it is P2.2's.** The parliamentary stop message says *"(b) if no results were found at all, synthesize your answer stating that no relevant records were found"*. Copied to legislation, it manufactures the bare negative P2.2 exists to stop. The dedupe key is `(changed_legislation, changed_provision, affecting_legislation, affecting_provision, type_of_effect)`, scheme-free by construction. **(2) The cap CAN be fetched past, cheaply, so the tool does that rather than stating a window.** Measured over the distinct `legislation_id`s the replay corpus actually touched (**272** at the last run): median **12** relation rows, p90 **870**, **13 (4.8%) over 2,000** — every one of which completes at 20,000 (largest 5,185 rows / 5.3 MB / 2.3 s). `ukpga/1988/1`, which the corpus never touches, reaches **13,681 rows / 11.9 MB**. So the fetch starts at 2,000, escalates once when exactly that many come back (which can only mean the cap bound), and states the window only if the second attempt also binds — never observed. **(3) `type_of_effect: null` is LABELLED, not dropped**, decided explicitly as the row asked: the row still records that an instrument changed a provision, so dropping it would make the tool the reason a relation went missing. Graded per TURN with `replay_report --dir <dir> commencements`, whose headline is a fact check against a ground truth `lex_probe --commencement` re-prints live. **Turns delivering the relation went 0 of 8 to 24 of 24; flat false negatives 6 of 8 to 0.** The before-column is zero in every wave — `baseline/` 0 of 8, `wave1/` 0 of 8, `wave2_p22_final/` 0 of 18 — because P2.2 changed the *shape* of the failure and not the outcome: *"No commencement regulations have been made yet"* became *"A search of the legislation index … did not return any results"*, honest and still not the answer. **Invariant 1 held in both directions.** Answers grew in **13 of 17** matched turn slots (6410 t2 87 -> 900 chars, 6409 t5 147 -> 944), no scope block leaked in any of 51 answers, and the four that shrank are all the model no longer padding a non-answer — 6409 t10 went from *"Are you certain of the SSI number and year?"* (**P2.4's exact failure**) to confirming the citation against the change record. **The discovery loop collapsed**: tool calls per answered turn **10.4 -> 4.4** across the same three sessions, with `search_legislation` 130 -> 101 over three times as many runs. **P2.3 did not regress**: 1 of 51 turns asserts an unverified derivation, against 2 of 29 in its own acceptance. **ONE TRAP THIS ROW LEFT OPEN, found at P2.5 (Session 9) and closed there:** `type_of_effect` has a **second** commencement string, `Commencement Order` (1,355 relations over the corpus's legislation_ids), and it is NOT the subject's commencement — its `changed_provision` is one of eight placeholder values and never a provision of the subject, because the row records another Act's commencement order bringing into force an **amendment to** the subject. `ukpga/1998/46` has 29 of them and zero `coming into force` rows, so a model asked what commenced the Scotland Act 1998 was handed 29 rows and a block inviting it to state any of them. | `[x]` | **P3.7** | **`/legislation/lookup` — a deterministic held/absent test, which keyword search cannot be.** *(New row from P2.2, Session 6, per the re-planning protocol — a retrieval fix, where P2.2 is the disclosure that has to stand until it lands.)* Four of the corpus's negatives are the same question: *is this instrument, named by number, in the index?* — 6409 turns 9-11 (`SSI 2025/377`, asked three times) and 6373 turn 2 (`SSI 2026/170`). All four were answered by `search_legislation`, a **ranked keyword search**, which cannot answer it: absence from the top 5 of a median 141 matches is not absence from the corpus, so the model reported a miss and then **asked the lawyer to check their own citation**. Both citations were right. **Both instruments are a 404 in LEX** (verified 2026-09-15), so the honest answer existed and no tool could reach it. `POST /legislation/lookup {legislation_type, year, number}` returns **200 or 404** — one of the ten endpoints P5.1 found we never call, and the only one that turns "I could not find it" into "it is not held". | `[x]` | **P4.2** | **B13 — Lost turns, blank replies, latency.** **15 user turns across 11 sessions never received a reply**; two assistant messages rendered blank — 6370 #8 **billed $0.0217** with nothing shown, 6363 #6 billed nothing. 6387 timed out on a follow-up. ~~n=2 on the blanks, so reproduce before fixing.~~ ~~**Reproduced in the P0.3 baseline: 4 billed-but-empty turns (6370 ×2, 6407 ×2), all `status: ok` with `audit.error: null`.**~~ **An undercount, corrected at the build (Session 10): there are NINE blank-body turns across the ten replay directories, EIGHT of them billed — 6370 ×2, 6406, 6407 ×2, 6338, 6359, 6383, plus 6374 r3 t3 at $0. 8 billed blanks in 616 turns (1.3%), six sessions, three chat modes. `python -m tools.replay_report --dir <dir> blanks`.** **Suspect (1) is eliminated by the evidence:** none of the four emitted a single `token` event, so there was never a body for the `<suggestions>` strip to consume. Start at suspect (2). Suspect (3) is unsupported — no error event on any of the four. **The acceptance test cannot name a turn:** this row cites 6370 #8, but the replay blanked at turns 2 and 3 while the pre-pilot's turn 2 answered normally — assert the invariant (non-empty body whenever recorded cost > 0), not a session-turn pair. The *timeout* half did **not** reproduce: 6387 answered all three turns. The billed-but-empty case is the informative one: content was generated and lost downstream. Eliminate in order: (1) a `<suggestions>` strip consuming the whole body — `utils/suggestions.py` takes the **last** block and also strips an *unterminated* trailing tag, which is exactly what a partial stream looks like; (2) a tool-call-only final message; (3) the `chat_loop` stream-retry guard re-raising after partial emission. **Suspect (4), added 2026-09-15 and now the leading one: the provider returns an empty final message and we cannot see that it did.** `chat_loop` (`agent/openrouter_client.py`) accumulates `delta.content` and `delta.tool_calls` and **never reads `finish_reason`**, so an empty stream becomes `{"role": "assistant", "content": ""}` and, with no tool calls, is returned as the answer with `status: ok`, no error and full billing. That is the B13 signature exactly, and it explains "no `token` event" without requiring anything to be lost downstream. Three public reports describe this shape on this model family: Google has **reproduced and is fixing** Gemini 3 streaming + function calling ending `finish_reason=STOP` with empty text after a tool executes -- absent in unary mode and on 2.5-flash (<https://discuss.ai.google.dev/t/gemini-3-5-flash-with-streamgeneratecontent-automatic-function-calling-stops-after-tool-execution-and-returns-empty-final-text/170528>); Gemini's `MALFORMED_FUNCTION_CALL` is silently normalised to `finish_reason='stop'` with empty content and zero tool calls, *indistinguishable from the model choosing to say nothing* (<https://github.com/BerriAI/litellm/issues/21744>); and OpenRouter emits duplicate `finish_reason` chunks on tool calls, the second one empty (<https://github.com/QwenLM/qwen-code/issues/2402>). OpenRouter's own docs add that models occasionally return no content during warm-up/scaling events, and that mid-stream failures arrive as `finish_reason: 'error'` carrying `native_finish_reason` (<https://openrouter.ai/docs/api_reference/errors-and-debugging>). **This is a lead, not a diagnosis** -- those reports are the google-genai SDK with *automatic* function calling and cite 3.5-flash / 3.1-flash-lite / 3.1-pro, not our OpenRouter manual tool loop on `google/gemini-3.1-pro-preview`. **So the first move is a diagnostic, not a fix:** capture `finish_reason` and `native_finish_reason` in `chat_loop` and surface them on the audit trace (additive, fail-soft, a few lines). Until that lands all four suspects look identical in the data, which is why this row has never been able to name a cause. It also means **the fix may not be ours** -- if it is the upstream streaming bug, the answer is a bounded retry on an empty final message, not a parser change. **New evidence from the Wave 1 sweep: 6338 turn 2** -- 2 delegations, real API calls, 330 s, $0.3996, `status: ok`, no `token` event, empty body. B13 is **not confined to 6370/6407**, which is what this row was scoped against. Separately, for perceived latency (MoniqueM reported a 5.5-minute turn as "around 15 minutes"), add a coarse step counter — the `tool_call` / `api_call_start` events already exist behind `emit_tool_details` and cost nothing in retrieval terms. **Acceptance** *(deterministic)*: assert a non-empty body whenever recorded cost > 0. **Evidence:** 6387 6370 6363 6345 6335 **6338** (Wave 1 sweep). **Depends on:** — (independent of the accuracy path) | **A NEW AND WORSE INSTANCE OF THIS BUCKET, found at P2.5 (Session 9), and the code has no guard against it.** 6383 rep 1 turn 4 of P2.5's acceptance sweep returned a Deep Research report whose body was **empty** - the lawyer saw the scope footer and nothing else - with `status: ok`, no error, 30 tool calls, three intact step reports (4,836 / 5,777 / 7,190 chars) and 219 commencement relations retrieved. **First blank in 218 answered turns across eight replay directories**, and 6383's DR turn produced 9,190 / 6,503 / 7,490 / 8,672 chars on the four prior runs. **The cause is an empty provider completion at the synthesis call**, not the scope strip: the request went out at `tools=0, msgs=2, ~21421 chars` and the task finished **9 seconds later**, and `strip_scope_blocks` never fired (it logs when it does; the log has no such line). **Two gaps this exposes, both additive to fix.** (1) `chat_loop`'s stream retry cannot catch it: it retries only while nothing has been emitted, and a successful 200 carrying no content chunks raises no exception, so an empty completion is indistinguishable from a finished answer. (2) `run_deep_research` never checks that the synthesis produced anything - a blank report is composed, footered and returned. The obvious fix is a fallback to the step findings when the synthesis comes back empty, which is strictly better than nothing and needs no new prompt. **n=1 and the re-run is the test**; P2.5's prompt change to `DEEP_RESEARCH_SYNTHESIS_PROMPT` is not excluded as a contributor, but no mechanism links a longer instruction to an empty completion and an empty 200 is known provider behaviour. **Evidence:** 6383. | **DONE (Session 10). The cause is an empty provider completion, and the code could not see one.** The diagnostic landed first as the row required: `finish_reason`, `native_finish_reason`, `reasoning_chars`, `completion_tokens` and a mid-stream `error` payload are now captured in both clients and surfaced as **audit schema v3's `empty_completions[]`** (`[]` on a healthy request; `AUDIT_TRACE.md` updated). They separate the four mechanisms the stored evidence could not tell apart — and the `error` payload is the one that was **ours**: OpenRouter reports a mid-stream failure that way and `chat_loop` read only `usage` and `choices`, so such a stream ended as an ordinary empty completion with `status: ok`. **The question the row posed about 6383 is answered and it changed the fix:** 6383's synthesis call declared NO tools (`tools=0, msgs=2`), so the failure is **not** confined to tool-call turns and a guard scoped to them would have missed it — the retry keys on an empty completion, full stop. It is stochastic, not deterministic (same payload succeeded five times, failed once), which is the condition a bounded retry answers. **Built:** (a) `chat_loop` in BOTH clients retries a clean 200 carrying no content and no tool calls, reusing `_MAX_STREAM_ATTEMPTS`/backoff; a tool-call-only message is never retried (that is the normal ReAct shape and replaying it re-runs the turn's research), and the discarded attempt's cost is banked so the turn's recorded spend stays honest. (b) `run_deep_research` falls back to the step findings, (c) the Manager falls back to the worker reports it already holds, or to a plain notice when there are none — **both labelled as fallbacks under Invariant 1**, since research the answering step never used is not an answer and must not read like one. **The latency half is built too:** a step count and an elapsed clock in the status line, both derived client-side from data already arriving (`tool_start` events, the run's existing `startedAt`) — no new SSE events, no backend change. **No denominator, deliberately**: outside Deep Research nothing knows the total, so "step 3 of 8" would be a claim about how much is left. **Acceptance PASSED:** `replay_report blanks` asserts the invariant over stored run files (exit 1 on a violation) and reproduces the hand count exactly on all ten directories. It grades the **body**, not the answer — since P2.2 a blank turn is 1,293 characters of scope footer with nothing above it, and grading `answer` scores that turn as fine. Validated both directions: the shortest bodies it leaves alone are real 46–50 char answers. **998 tests** (963 → 998). Live-verified end to end: a real research turn returned a full report, and the status line read *"Researching · 13 steps · 1m 01s"*. **B13 closes.** **Residual found at P2.8 (Session 13), now row P4.5:** the retry and both fallbacks exist at the Manager and Deep Research seams, but not at the WORKER's. A worker whose final completion is lost hands the Manager a report with no body.

| `[x]` | **P4.10** | **A lost completion is expensive: about 63,000 reasoning tokens per attempt, and the retry pays it again.** *(New row, end of Session 26, from P4.5 by user decision, option (c): P4.5 keeps the labelling, this row takes the cost.)* On the pinned model, a completion that ends `finish_reason=stop` with no content has cost about 62,912 completion (reasoning) tokens per attempt, and P4.2's bounded retry makes up to three. **Measured episodes (all on P4.5's row):** `wave0_conv_6348`/6348 rep 2 turn 1, $2.40 and 1,133 s against $0.07 and 33 s for rep 1; `wave3_p313` rep 2 turn 1, $2.48 and 1,171 s, and rep 3 turn 3, $2.40 and 1,120 s, together $4.89 of that directory's $5.65; `wave3_p38`/6383 r1 t3, $1.62 and 768 s for a 1,429-char answer. Some of these turns still answered, so the defect is latency and cost, not correctness; P4.5 covers the answers that lost a step. **Candidate levers, none chosen:** (i) cap the reasoning budget on Worker calls (OpenRouter's `reasoning` parameter, `max_tokens` or `effort`); (ii) do not retry a completion that came back empty after heavy reasoning, or retry it with the budget lowered; (iii) a wall-clock or token ceiling per worker call that fails into P4.5's label. Each touches every worker call, so each can cost answer quality. **Measure first:** recount the episodes, their cost and wall clock over all 49 directories (`replay_report blanks` counts calls and attempts; add cost and seconds if it does not print them). Check whether the provider honours a reasoning cap on this model before designing around one. **Acceptance** *(replay)*: to be chosen with the lever and booked here before any build. It must show the cost and latency of affected turns falling, and Invariant 1 (links, `sources_kept`, depth on the graded sessions) not falling on unaffected ones. **Evidence:** the episodes above; P4.5's row. **USER DECISION (Session 28, 2026-09-25): lever (ii) plus a 32,000-token output cap on Worker calls. The affected-turns half is evidenced by a seam A/B plus unit tests, with no replay acceptance.** **PRE-FLIGHT, behind commands** (`replay_report --dir <any> lostcost --all-dirs`, `seam_replay worker --as-sent`, `reasoning_probe`; `23ac8e4` and this commit; the tables are in BASELINE.md, "The cost of a lost completion"). Over 50 directories and 930 schema-v3 turns, the 34 empty calls split into four mechanisms. **(a) reasoned to nothing:** 8 calls (15 attempts), 2 recovered, every one in a research Worker and every one in the conversational quick-lookup Worker: 8 of 478 conversational worker runs against 0 of 522 in the other chat modes (one-sided Fisher 0.0026; 4 of the 8 are session 6348, so they come from 5 sessions). The 8 turns cost $12.38 and 7,073 s over their slots' clean medians (median per turn $1.33 and 902 s), against $156.55 for all v3 turns. **(b) upstream idle timeout:** 11 calls (32 attempts), 3 recovered, $0.29 and 3,143 s over the medians (median 371 s): a latency cost, not a spend one. **(c) clean stop, no reasoning:** 2 calls, both Deep Research steps, no excess. **(d) upstream rate limit** (new): 13 calls, all recovered, no excess. The retry after an (a) attempt answered 2 times in 12 (after (b) 3 in 22, after (d) 13 in 14), and the Manager's re-delegation made good 6 of 6 unrecovered (a) worker calls. **Probes.** The model's output ceiling is 65,536 tokens and its reasoning is mandatory. With no `reasoning` field it reasons in the `high` range, about twice `medium`. `reasoning.max_tokens` lands on an effort level, not a budget. A top-level `max_tokens` binds. On the seam, both `wave3_p313`/6348 payloads (r2 t1, r3 t3) rebuild to exactly their recorded `sent_chars`, and both re-drew a runaway to 62,916 reasoning tokens: r2 t1 came back empty (370 s, $0.77), r3 t3 answered after 310 s ($0.77). **With `max_tokens` 16,000 the r3 t3 draw came back empty at 15,360 tokens (96% of the cap, as 62,912 is of 65,536) in 93 s for $0.20. A cap ends the failure sooner; it does not make the model answer.** Lowering the effort did not bound it: on r2 t1, 3 of 4 draws at `medium` or `low` came back empty (two (a), one (b)), and the one answer missed s.36(2). **Spend on the pre-flight: $4.35** ($0.93 on the tiny probe calls, including the committed re-run; $3.42 on seam draws). **ACCEPTANCE BOOKED (Session 28, 2026-09-25, before any product code).** **Build.** (1) `chat_loop` in both clients takes `worker_call` (default False) and forwards it through its ReAct recursion and P3.8's write-up round. `run_worker_agent` passes True on its research loop only, so the Manager, the planner, the Deep Research synthesis and the A4 reformat are unchanged; a Deep Research step is a worker run and is covered. (2) Lever (ii), both clients: on a Worker call, an empty completion that reasoned heavily (`is_heavy_empty`: 10,000 or more completion tokens, or 20,000 or more reasoning characters, the instrument's own (a) threshold) is not retried. It returns empty at once into P4.5's lost-report label, and its record says `retried: false`. A light empty ((b), (c), (d)) is retried as before, and a non-worker call retries every empty as before. (3) The cap, OpenRouter only: a Worker call's payload carries `max_tokens: 32000` (`WORKER_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS`), and every other payload is unchanged. Ollama accepts the flag and applies (ii) only, because `num_predict` on its cloud models is unmeasured. (4) A capped call that returns content with `finish_reason=length` is logged as possibly cut (a watch item; the bound below says it should be rare). No audit shape change: P4.5's label, `delegations[].lost` and schema v6 are untouched. **Pass conditions.** (a) *Deterministic:* unit tests at each seam, each shown to fail with its product change reverted on a scratch copy. They cover the payload with and without the flag in both clients; the retry decision for each mechanism, with and without the flag; the flag forwarded through the recursion and the write-up round; `run_worker_agent` passing it, and the Manager, synthesis, planner and reformat not; and a worker whose heavy empty is not retried returning P4.5's label after one attempt. (b) *Affected turns, seam A/B:* `seam_replay worker --as-sent` on `wave3_p313`/6348 r2 t1 and r3 t3, three draws a side. The lever-off side is the product payload; the lever-on side is `--max-tokens 32000`. Pass: no lever-on draw exceeds 32,000 completion tokens, and on each payload the lever-on median cost and median seconds are below the lever-off medians. With (ii), an (a) episode makes one attempt, so its worst case is one capped attempt (at most $0.384: 32,000 tokens at $12 per million) plus the Manager's re-delegation, against today's median of $1.33 and 902 s over the slot. The figures booked are those draws, not an estimate. A lever-on draw that answers is graded (links, depth) and reported. (c) *Unaffected turns (Invariant 1), seam A/B, one draw a side, three a side for any flagged payload:* the clean turn with the longest worker window in each chat mode, which `lostcost` names (`wave0_conv_6348_pre`/6348 r2 t3, `wave2`/6341 r1 t7, `wave4_p46_pre`/p46_6385 r1 t4), and `wave3_p313`/6348 r1 t1. A lever-off draw under 32,000 completion tokens is one the cap cannot bind; there the lever-on draw must keep its outcome, links and depth grade. A lever-off draw over 32,000 is an affected call and is reported as one. Supporting, not a pass condition: by cost, a 32,000-token call cannot have occurred in 100% of 533 clean conversational turns or in 96% of 284 clean research turns (Deep Research cannot be bounded this way, because its turns sum many calls), and a clean turn's longest worker window exceeded 180 s in 1 turn per mode (of 425, 83 and 181). **No replay acceptance**, by the user's decision: a lost completion is 1.7% of v3 turns and cannot be provoked. **Budget:** about $6 on the seam. **BUILT, ACCEPTANCE OPEN (Session 28, 2026-09-25). The row stays `[~]` by user decision.** Built in `df95c09` on the acceptance above (`484f32f`). **(a) PASSED:** `tests/test_lost_cost.py`, 16 tests. Each product file, reverted alone to `484f32f` on a scratch copy, fails them: `empty_completion.py` at import, `openrouter_client.py` 9, `ollama_client.py` 2, `research_halt.py` 1, `agent_core.py` 2. 49 test fakes gained `worker_call=False`. when a reply arrives paste it into `SESSION_LOG.md` and tick the row — and for P5.1 record the

| `[x]` | **P5.1** | **B3(a) — Ask the LEX team whether relationships are retrievable.** Does the API expose enabling-power, amendment, commencement or revocation relations in any form? This determines whether the largest bucket is **buildable or permanently a disclosure**, and P2.3 is the holding fix either way. Known from direct probing: `/legislation/search`, `/legislation/section/search` and `/legislation/text` are the only endpoints we call; `text` returns a record for `ssi/2025/119` with an **empty `text` field** while `section/search` returns nothing for it, so stub records exist and are indistinguishable from absence at the tool boundary — worth raising in the same conversation. **Acceptance** *(external)*: a recorded answer in `SESSION_LOG.md`, and either a new row or a note that P2.3 is permanent. **Depends on:** — | **ANSWERED 2026-09-15 (Session 5), and largely without them.** The API publishes an OpenAPI spec at **`/openapi.json`** listing **13 endpoints; we call 3**. Nobody had looked. **Amendment, commencement, repeal and revocation are all retrievable** via `/amendment/search` and `/amendment/section/search`: provision-to-provision, a resolvable URL on both sides, a `type_of_effect` vocabulary (246 "coming into force", 43 "Commencement Order", 62 "repealed", 6 "revoked" over 1,358 sampled rows), and a **`search_amended` flag that carries the direction**. **Only *made under* has no route** — not in `/legislation/lookup`'s fields, and explanatory notes are Act-level (404 for `ssi/2020/295`). **So B3 is BUILDABLE, not permanently a disclosure**, for four of its five relations → **P3.5**; P2.3 narrows to enabling power alone. Separately: `description` on every search result states relationships in prose *with dates* and `_slim_search_results` discards it → **P3.6**. **The stub question in this row was wrong and is corrected:** `/legislation/text` returns `{legislation, full_text}`, the nested `text` key is empty for *everything* including fully-held Acts, and a genuine stub is explicitly signalled (`full_text` = "No text content available for this legislation.", plus `/legislation/section/lookup` 404) — stub, held and absent are three distinguishable states. Evidence is reproducible: `python -m tools.lex_probe`. **Five narrower questions remain for the LEX team** (`WAVE5_QUESTIONS.md`), led by enabling power and the missing commencement date. **Acceptance met** — answer recorded in `SESSION_LOG.md` and here.
| `[~]` | **P5.2** | **B12 — The Scottish case-law corpus.** The Court of Session and Sheriff Courts are not in the National Archives corpus.
