This is a product/procurement question, not an engineering one; P2.4 is the engineering half and is not blocked by it. **Record it as the top substantive content gap of the pre-pilot.** **Acceptance** *(external)*: a recorded decision. **Depends on:** — | **A lead found 2026-09-15 (Session 5) that may change this from procurement to an API request.** LEX's own `/healthcheck` reports case-law collections in its vector store — **`caselaw` 69,970 points, `caselaw_section` 4,723,735, `caselaw_summary` 61,107** — while `/openapi.json` exposes **no case-law endpoint at all**. A case-law corpus therefore exists inside a system we already call, and we reach case law through the National Archives instead. **Ask whether it is reachable and whether it includes the Court of Session and the Sheriff Courts before treating this as a purchase.** Reproduce: `python -m tools.lex_probe --coverage`. **FACTS ESTABLISHED 2026-09-15 (Session 5); the decision itself is not mine and the row stays open.** **The gap is verified, and it is narrower than "no Scots law":** TNA offers **42 court codes, none Scottish and none Northern Irish** (scraped from the search facet), and `?court=csoh`/`csih` are rejected **400 — "csoh is not one of the available choices"**. **But Scottish appeals that reached the UK Supreme Court ARE indexed** — *Daly v HM Advocate (Scotland)*, *ABC v Principal Reporter (Scotland)*, *X v Lord Advocate*. So what is missing is **the Court of Session, Sheriff Appeal Court, Sheriff Courts and High Court of Justiciary** — where the overwhelming majority of Scots law is actually made — not Scots law entirely. State it that way: the blunt version is false and would make a lawyer distrust a sound UKSC result. **And the failure mode is a wrong answer, not an empty one:** a `query=Court of Session` search returns 200 with 50 results of which **not one is a Scottish judgment** (20 EWHC, 11 UKFTT, 5 EWFC, 4 UKSC, 3 UKUT, 3 EWCA, 2 EWCOP, 1 EAT) — English authority that merely mentions Scotland, which the model then answers from. That is 6375 precisely, and it **raises P2.4's priority**: an empty result is a disclosure, a full one of the wrong jurisdiction is a trap. **Every court AILA can filter on is non-Scottish** — 14 options in the UI and the tool schema, none Scottish (the codes are correct; this is the product gap, not a filter defect). ~~**Ask the LEX team before buying anything:** … if that corpus covers the Court of Session this is an API request to a team we already work with.~~ **Largely closed off by user knowledge, 2026-09-15 — and the row's real subject is now FINDING AN ALTERNATIVE CASE-LAW API.** The probe found `/healthcheck` reporting `caselaw` **69,970**, `caselaw_section` **4,723,735** and `caselaw_summary` **61,107** points while `/openapi.json` exposes no case-law endpoint and the conventional mirror names all **404**. **The user recalls that LEX *used to* expose case law and has since DISABLED access, and that Scottish courts were not represented in that data anyway.** *(Recollection, recorded as such — not verified. It is consistent with what the probe found, which cannot distinguish "disabled" from "never exposed".)* Two consequences: the LEX question drops from "is this reachable" to a **confirmation** worth one line in the email, and even a yes would probably not deliver the Court of Session. **So the substantive work is sourcing case law elsewhere**, which is a different question from the licence one this row was written around. **Candidates, none assessed:** **BAILII** carries `ScotCS` (Court of Session), `ScotHC` (High Court of Justiciary) and `ScotSC` (Sheriff Court) and is the obvious technical fit — **but check its terms of use before designing anything, as they restrict automated access, and discovering that late would waste the build**; the **Scottish Courts and Tribunals Service** (`scotcourts.gov.uk`) publishes opinions directly and should be checked for a feed or API; and the commercial providers (Westlaw, LexisNexis) which are a procurement question with a licence cost. Whatever is chosen must also clear the **internet-restricted target's whitelist** — see `CLAUDE.md` → External API Dependencies — which rules out anything needing broad outbound access. Brief and the decision as three steps: `WAVE5_QUESTIONS.md`. **P2.4 is not blocked by any of it** — whatever the corpus holds, an answer drawing on English authority for a Scots-law question must say so, in code, every time.
| `[x]` | **P5.3** | **Corpus currency.** Verified absent from LEX in September 2026: Education (Scotland) Act 1962 (`ukpga/1962/47` → 404), SSI 2026/170 → 404, SSI 2025/119 (record exists, text empty), stub-only provisions for the Education (Scotland) Act 1945. Ask what the index's coverage rules and refresh cadence actually are, so B5's "absent from the index" wording can be accurate rather than generic. **Acceptance** *(external)*: a recorded answer. **Depends on:** — | **ANSWERED 2026-09-15 (Session 5), the same way as P5.1 — `GET /api/stats`, `GET /healthcheck` and direct coverage sampling; `python -m tools.lex_probe --coverage`.** **The index is refreshed DAILY and is current** — ingestion runs land ~02:00–02:30 UTC and the newest `created_at` observed was **today, 02:17**. Corpus: **220,022** instruments, 2,107,361 provisions, 2,580,915 amendments. **So staleness is not the problem; per-instrument gaps are, and for 2026 they are severe:** ASP 2025/26 **100%**, SSI 2025 **85%**, ~~**SSI 2026 5%**, **UK SI 2026 0%** (20-point samples)~~, UKPGA 1962 **60%**. **Two of those five were corrected at P2.2 (Session 6) by the cross-check this row itself prescribes, and one of them in the direction that matters.** On a 60-point census: SSI 2026 **2%**, UK SI 2026 **2%**, SSI 2025 **87%** — and "UK SI 2026 0%" is the number to retire, because it *reads* as "nothing from 2026 is held", which is false: `/legislation/search` returns **27 distinct `uksi/2026/*` instruments**, and all 12 spot-checked resolve on `/legislation/lookup`. Low single digits, not nothing. The product wording that came out of this row therefore says "under 5%" and never "none" — a lawyer told "none" stops looking. All three original claims re-verified: `ukpga/1962/47` absent — **and it is a per-instrument gap, not a date cliff**, since `/41 /42 /45 /50 /51 /55` are all held, so "we don't hold pre-19xx" is the wrong story to tell a lawyer; `ssi/2026/170` absent, consistent with ~5–20% coverage of that series; `ssi/2025/119` an explicit stub. **This directly sets B5's wording (P2.2): on these numbers a "not found" for a 2026 SSI is far more likely a coverage gap than an absence in law**, and a negative that does not say so is close to telling a lawyer the instrument does not exist. **Method warning, and it cost two wrong conclusions in this probe:** point lookups are a bad census instrument — twelve misses across `ssi/2026/{1..250}` read as "no 2026 SSIs at all", and `uksi/2026/772` was in the index the whole time, created a week earlier. Cross-check a lookup census against `/legislation/search`. **One finding belongs to P5.2, not here:** `/healthcheck` reports **case-law collections** (`caselaw` 69,970, `caselaw_section` 4,723,735, `caselaw_summary` 61,107) that **no endpoint in `/openapi.json` exposes**. **Remaining for the LEX team** (`WAVE5_QUESTIONS.md`): why is 2026 only ~20% ingested when the refresh is daily; is the older patchiness inherited from legislation.gov.uk; is there a published coverage statement. **Also now on the list, for the National Archives rather than LEX: what is the case-law corpus's DATE coverage?** Judgments exist back to **1965**, but neither the Atom feed (capped at 50 per query, every window) nor the search page exposes a total, so density before ~2000 could not be measured here — see the Session 5 log, where an unverified "coverage starts ~2001" claim was made and retracted. CambeulW's recency-bias complaint (6363) may well be sound; it is currently **unmeasured**, and it would affect English research too, not only Scots.

| *(no bucket)* | P2.7 (discovery budget; unblocks P3.1 and P4.1), P3.8 (same-resource retrieval, and the halted worker's lost findings), P3.9 (case-law date filter), P3.10 (dependent Deep Research steps), P4.10 (the cost of a lost completion; P4.5 keeps the labelling), P4.11 (an upstream idle timeout's latency; P4.10's residual), P4.12 (the Manager's idle timeout, and a retry with different bytes; P4.11's residual) |

Thomas sent nine proposed actions ("Nine changes to make LexChat's answers more

was checked against this branch, the replay directories or the live service. The
document itself is not committed: `2026-09-10-lexchat-developer-actions.md`, supplied by the user in

Everything this plan uses from it is on the rows below.

| 3 Failed research vs a completed report | **P2.1**, **P2.6** built; **P4.5** built (the lost step labelled; 6363; the progress event); **P3.8** (write-up at the cap); new **P3.10** (dependent steps) |

**Held as to be verified — not rows until the evidence exists:**
- **Case-law relevance ordering (action 6).** It changed the result count from 50 to
  10 in Thomas's check. Measure coverage before adopting it; keep it out of P3.9.
- **Summary counts that contradict the answer's own list ("not here").** No instance
  in our classification. Wait for one.
- **A literal "£" scan over whole instruments (action 4).** It serves one question

## Merging back

`main` is the deployment branch and the target pulls `origin/main`.

**Nothing is pushed to `main` until the whole plan is concluded (user decision, 2026-09-15).**
~~Waves are independently mergeable and should be merged as they complete rather than held to
the end.~~ That was the original policy and it is no longer in force: the branch is completed
in full and merged once, in a single push.

**Consequence a session must not talk itself out of.** The target keeps running the pre-pilot
code until that push, including B2 — measured at P1.5 as emptying 351 of 974 filtered searches
and costing 28% more and 38% longer per query. That is understood and accepted; it is not an
argument for pushing Wave 1 early, and a later session should not re-open it. If the delay
becomes untenable, raise it with the user rather than deciding unilaterally.

**"Concluded" means Waves 0–4** — the code work. Wave 5 is external, has unknown lead time,
and the plan already states those rows are not blockers for anything in Waves 0–4; they must
not hold the merge hostage to a third party's reply.

The frontend build rule still applies: any change under `client/src/` needs `npm run build` and
a force-added `client/dist/` in the same commit (see the root `CLAUDE.md`).
